# Decision Log — Prediction Market Trading Bot

**Purpose:** Chronological index of all decisions (ADRs, approvals, and notable judgment calls) made during this project. Single source of truth for "why did we decide X."  
**Owner (role):** Director/Sponsor (DS)  
**Last reviewed:** 2026-06-27 SESSION 90 CLOSE — MLB-G1 gate closed (T-194 through T-199 COMPLETE). T-194: `mark_aged_open()` in-place tagging (position stays in open_positions, preserves bankroll invariant). T-195: permissive-with-logging pattern for silent exception handlers. T-197: kill switch drill APR-0023 (halt_time_ms=0.116, SLA PASS). GG-043/GG-044 registered. ADR-0032 ACCEPTED. 6 MLB crons enabled on VPS. 2044/2044 tests.  
**Next review:** G5 Operational Gate; any new ADR/approval  
**Staleness triggers:** New ADR accepted; approval decision made; major judgment call not captured elsewhere  
**Upstream links:** 03_DECISIONS/  
**Downstream links:** 00_ADMIN/APPROVALS_REGISTER.md, all ADRs  
**Decision references:** ADR-0001, ADR-0002  
**Confidence:** 9/10 — Current decisions fully logged; completeness depends on ongoing discipline

Evidence quality unchanged. If not reached by Finals end, window extends to first 30 days of 2026-27 regular season. **⚠ STALE ASSUMPTION — see 2026-05-31 corrective entry below.** | DS |
| 2026-05-31 | **[CORRECTION TO APR-0016]** "4 bets/game-day max" assumption is invalidated by ADR-0006 — G6 evidence window is shorter than APR-0016 estimated | APR-0016 was written 2026-05-29, two days before ADR-0006 deleted `max_concurrent_positions: 10`. The "4 bets/game-day" figure was derived from the count-gate-era system — a system where a hard ceiling of 10 simultaneous positions silently suppressed bet accumulation. ADR-0006 rationale explicitly states: "If the bot works as designed, thousands of trades per game should flow through the pipeline." Corrected throughput model: bet count per game night is determined by (cohort players in game) × (Kalshi markets per player) × (edge approval rate) — no count ceiling. Conservative estimate for a Finals game (~24-30 cohort players, ~2 markets each, ~35% approval rate): 17-40+ bets per game night. The 30-bet G6 evidence threshold may be reached in the first 1-2 Finals game nights, not across the full Finals series. The 30-bet threshold itself is correct and unchanged — only the timeline estimate was wrong. Dark period fallback (2026-27 regular season) is now significantly less likely to be needed. | DS |

Rather than waiting, use the window for: Polymarket activation (T-077), DS-04 qualitative source (T-073), ADR-0005 live capital draft, Phase 8 preparation. This is an explicit strategic sequencing decision — do not treat dark period as idle time. | DS |

| 2026-05-29 | T-072 model refresh elevated from "optional" to required before 2026-27 paper trading resumes | KI-013: model v1-28483b21 trained through 2026-05; 5+ months stale at 2026-27 season open. Paper bets in early 2026-27 with a stale model are lower-quality G6 evidence. Retrain by 2026-10-15 (before regular season tip-off). New ADR required per ADR-0004. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Adopt 11-strategy architecture (A–K) as project strategy roadmap | Edge source diversification across all four edge types (Type 1–4); sequenced by infrastructure complexity and governance gate against Phase gates G6–G9. Strategy A buildable immediately; K is Phase 8+. Full mechanics, risk profiles, and activation gates documented in STRATEGY_OVERVIEW.md. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Kelly Criterion chosen over flat betting for all position sizing | Kelly maximizes long-run bankroll growth rate given a known edge. Flat betting ignores edge magnitude entirely, leading to systematic undersizing on high-edge bets and oversizing on low-edge bets. Fractional Kelly mitigates calibration uncertainty inherent in any model-based system. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Quarter-Kelly (0.25×) set as default Kelly fraction (DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION=0.25) | Full Kelly requires a perfectly calibrated model — any real model has estimation error. Quarter-Kelly delivers ~56% of full Kelly long-run growth rate while dramatically reducing variance and ruin probability. Industry standard for model-based trading. Confirmed by ADR-0004 (LR+Platt ECE 0.0342) as the correct operating point. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Hard bankroll limits set: $500/bet cap, $1,000/day max loss, 20% drawdown kill switch trigger | Conservative limits calibrated to $1,000 paper bankroll. $500/bet cap prevents single-event market impact at Kalshi's thin liquidity. $1,000/day max loss = full bankroll ruin floor. 20% drawdown triggers kill switch per NFR-03 (APR-0013). All three limits must be formally revised via ADR at G6 when live bankroll is set. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** T1–T5 execution band framework adopted as the time-horizon strategy architecture | Five distinct decision windows exist in NBA prediction markets (T1: 12–48h multi-day; T2: 4–8h lineup; T3: 60min pre-tip; T4: in-play; T5: final 5min). Derived from HFT principle: model complexity is inversely proportional to execution urgency. Each band has defined compute budget, SLA, and Kelly fraction. T4/T5 blocked until Phase 8 real-time infrastructure. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Kelly fraction schedule by execution band: T1=0.25, T2=0.20, T3=0.10, T4=0.05, T5=0.15 | T1 uses full quarter-Kelly (ADR-0004 calibration applies). T2 reduced to 0.20 because injury/lineup overlay adds uncertainty to T1 estimates. T3 reduced to 0.10 because momentum signal is a T1 confirmation filter, not an independent model. T4 reduced to 0.05 because no historical calibration exists for live Bayesian model. T5 elevated to 0.15 above T4 because arithmetic near-certainty breaks the probabilistic horizon rule — T5 is not a shorter-horizon probabilistic model but deterministic arithmetic. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Multi-band interaction rules adopted: T3>T2>T1 priority; CONFIRM/FADE/NEUTRAL protocol | When multiple bands fire on the same market, the most recent band (T3) overrides because it reflects the most current information. CONFIRM signal applies a 1.15× edge multiplier (momentum confirms T1 direction). FADE signal holds the position (do not place against confirmed T1 signal). NEUTRAL proceeds at base band sizing. Implemented in T-084 (execution_bands_orchestrator.py). | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** Phase 7 strategy activation priority order: E→B→I→C | Line Shopping (E) is first: highest confidence (8/10), lowest infrastructure delta (VenueRouter already built). Data Aggregation (B) is continuous improvement alongside E. Poisson/NB (I) is third: requires ScoringDistributionModel EXP to validate before build (LL-009 pilot-first rule). Complement Arb (C) is fourth: blocked on APR-0010 + C5 write-capable key + two-leg execution. EXP-014 confirmed E adds +7.8% max EV improvement. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | **[RETROACTIVE]** EXP-014 line shopping result (+7.8% max EV improvement) elevated Strategy E to first post-G6 build priority | EXP-014 confirmed that routing to the best-price venue adds +7.8% to maximum achievable EV vs. single-venue execution. This moved Strategy E from a Phase 7 theoretical consideration to the first concrete build target after G6. VenueRouter (T-083) was built in the same sprint in anticipation. | DS |
| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 19 / run_t4_live.py]** Bug H drawdown fix applied to T4 live; same pattern as PIPE-03 | `run_t4_live.py` computed drawdown as `(initial_bankroll - current_bankroll) / initial_bankroll`. After T1 deploys ~$540, this reads as ~54% drawdown — above the 20% C4 kill switch — silently rejecting every T4 bet before the first whistle. Fix: `_realized_loss = max(0.0, -portfolio.get("total_realized_pnl", 0.0))`. Deployed capital is not a loss until the position resolves. During an active game `total_realized_pnl = 0.0` (no closed positions), so drawdown is correctly 0.0%. Same two-line pattern as PIPE-03 Bug H fix (SESSION 14). 695/695 tests on VPS. Commit `706264b`. | DS |
| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 20 / ADR-0013 / KI-026]** T4 band budget architecture: Kelly scoped to t4_band_budget, not full portfolio bankroll | Standard Kelly on full bankroll ($618) yields $6.18/bet × ~150 T4 fires/game = ~$927 capital demand, exceeding the entire portfolio. ADR-0013 fixes this: `t4_band_budget = bankroll × (1 - RESERVE_MANDATE_PCT=0.40) × T4_BAND_WEIGHT=0.10` = $37 at current bankroll. Kelly operates on $37 → $0.37/bet floored to $1 paper minimum. Band-level kill switch: budget exhausted → T4 halts, T1 unaffected. System-level kill switches unchanged. Two stub constants (T4_BAND_WEIGHT=0.10, T4_EXPECTED_TOTAL_EDGE=5.0) require G1 PAPER_T4_VIRTUAL calibration before T4 ACTIVE. Verified: `scripts/run_t4_budget_sim.py` (budget exhausts at cycle 125/200 with 37 bets at current bankroll; architecture scales correctly to any capital ceiling). T1 Monte Carlo unaffected. PAPER_T4_VIRTUAL=true remains default. See ADR-0013 for activation gates. | DS |
| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 19 / run_t4_live.py]** PAPER_T4_VIRTUAL=true adopted as default for paper T4 proof-of-architecture; does not deduct bankroll | T4 Kelly at 0.20 gives ~$100–120/bet at $600 bankroll. Unlimited real-capital T4 bets would deplete T1 capital pool within 5–6 fires. Paper T4 purpose is to prove the architecture fires at signal scale, not to compete with T1 for capital. PAPER_T4_VIRTUAL=true: C4 evaluates + approves the bet, `staged_orders.db` records it, logger emits "T4 VIRTUAL" line — but `trader.open_position()` is NOT called. Bankroll is unaffected. Virtual bets prove ~1000 evaluation cycles/game are architecturally reachable without capital depletion. Disable with `PAPER_T4_VIRTUAL=false` when T4 capital-constrained sizing model is finalized (see KI-026 below). Env var; no ADR required (paper-only mechanism). | DS |
| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 19]** T4 capital-constrained bet sizing is an open architectural question; must be resolved before T4 goes ACTIVE | Design intent: T4 fires ~1000 evaluation cycles/game WITHIN the capital ceiling (whatever it is — $1,000, $600, or $1M). This is not a license to ignore the ceiling. At T4 Kelly=0.20 and $600 bankroll, a single approved bet is ~$120 — five bets deplete the T4-accessible capital. High-frequency in-play execution (HRT analogy: many small trades, not a few large ones) requires a fundamentally different per-bet sizing model than pregame Kelly. No experiment or ADR currently addresses this. EXP-018 scope is `T4_MIN_DELTA_P` calibration — separate from sizing. A sizing model for continuous in-play firing must be registered as a task and resolved via ADR before T4 ACTIVE designation. Until then, PAPER_T4_VIRTUAL=true is the correct paper mode. | DS |
| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 19]** Finals schedule pinned from ESPN; "Finals Game 1" / "gate G6" disambiguation standard adopted | ESPN confirmed: Finals Game 1 = NYK at SAS, Jun 3 PDT 8:30 PM EDT / 00:30 UTC Jun 4. Full schedule: Finals Game 1 Jun 3 PDT, Finals Game 2 Jun 5 PDT, Finals Game 3 Jun 8 PDT, Finals Game 4 Jun 10 PDT. Gate G6 (live capital authorization gate) and Finals Game 1 are hereafter spelled out in full — never abbreviated to the same "G1"/"G6" format in the same context, as they have historically been confused. ESPN internal game IDs (40185XXXX format) are banned from human-readable documentation — code only. | DS |
| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 19]** Portfolio reset recommendation for Finals Game 1: do not reset; let game run and resolve | Current portfolio: $459.47 remaining, 11 open positions for Jun 3 game. 4 positions placed successfully (KAT 3 thresholds + Fox YES 10); 3 Kalshi-canceled (Fox NO 25, Fox NO 20, Anunoby NO 25); 4 failed HTTP 503/429 (Wembanyama 3 thresholds, Castle 1 threshold). All 11 positions are under the corrected Path B (Poisson CDF) architecture. Resetting now wipes the first real Finals game evidence. All positions will resolve via run_resolve_local.py after the game using actual player stats — Kalshi order status is irrelevant to paper resolution. Recommendation: run Finals Game 1, resolve after, let EXP-017 n grow. Present fresh reset as an option for Finals Game 2 if needed. | DS |
| 2026-06-03 | **[SESSION 18 / PIPE-07]** ESPN API adopted as primary live data source for T4 in-play polling; NBA CDN permanently replaced | NBA Stats CDN (`cdn.nba.com`, `stats.nba.com`) is blocked on VPS (Digital Ocean cloud IPs) by Akamai WAF — returns "Access Denied" HTML with 403 for all nba_api requests. Confirmed via direct curl from VPS. ESPN API (`site.api.espn.com/apis/site/v2/sports/basketball/nba/`) is unblocked and returns valid JSON. Decision: replace `NBALiveFeed` with `_espn_poll_once()` for all T4/T5 live game data. Implementation: `_espn_game_ids_today()` (scoreboard discovery), `_parse_espn_clock()` (period + displayClock → minutes remaining), `_espn_poll_once()` (boxscore → `LivePlayerState` list). Player matching: `name_to_pregame` dict keyed by `name.lower().replace(" ", "")`. ESPN player stats format: `boxscore.players[].statistics[0].labels` (parallel array with `athletes[].stats`). This is a DS-02 source selection, not a protocol change — T4EventLoop, C4, C5, C6 are unchanged. Permanent until a better non-CDN live data source is identified. `run_t4_live.py` commits: `47e9a2b` (initial build), `2446a02` (ESPN main loop fix). | DS |
| 2026-06-03 | **[SESSION 18 / risk.py]** MIN_BET_USD paper-mode override added to RiskEngine as init param; paper T4 uses $1.0 floor | `MIN_BET_USD = 10.0` in `_HARD_LIMITS` reflects the live capital minimum for meaningful execution. In paper T4 mode, $10 floor blocks nearly all Kelly-sized bets at small bankroll — suppressing evidence accumulation without protecting capital (paper mode has no real capital). Fix: `min_bet_usd: float | None = None` param on `RiskEngine.__init__()`. When `None`, falls back to `MIN_BET_USD` (live behavior unchanged). `run_t4_live.py` instantiates with `min_bet_usd=MIN_BET_T4_PAPER=1.0`. This does NOT modify `_HARD_LIMITS` — no ADR required. Paper path only. Committed as part of `47e9a2b`. | DS |
| 2026-06-03 | **[SESSION 18 / PIPE-07]** Ghost protection ordering adopted: staged_order_store.stage_order() BEFORE paper_trader.open_position() in T4 cycle | Two-phase write ordering in `_run_cycle()`: (1) `store.stage_order()` — SQLite WAL ACID write to `staged_orders.db`; (2) `trader.open_position()` — JSON write to `paper_portfolio.json`; (3) `store.mark_fired()` — status update. If the JSON write (step 2) fails, the SQLite record remains as a reconciliation anchor — the next cycle can detect the orphaned STAGED record. Reverse ordering (JSON before SQLite) would leave no trace if JSON write fails. This matches the PIPE-03 pre-staging design intent from SESSION 12. | DS |
| 2026-06-03 | **[SESSION 18 / PIPE-07]** Per-cycle idempotency only in T4: fired_tickers resets each 45s cycle; cross-cycle re-entry is intentional | `fired_tickers: set[str]` in `_run_cycle()` prevents double-fire of the same ticker within one 45s evaluation cycle. It is NOT persisted across cycles. Design intent: T4 should re-evaluate the same ticker in the next cycle if prices move or game state changes — a changed `p_t4` after 45s is a legitimate new signal, not a duplicate. Cross-cycle idempotency would suppress this and defeat the purpose of continuous in-play polling. The staged_order_store (SQLite STAGED/FIRED statuses) provides durable deduplication for the session level if needed. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[T-118]** Apply all-threshold loop fix to run_pregame.py (EXP-017 data pipeline completeness) | `run_pregame.py` retained `closest = min(player_mkts, ...)` after T-115 fixed `run_paper_trade.py`. Pregame logs are the EXP-017 Part B data source — single-threshold logs captured only 1 of ~7–8 Kalshi markets per player, restricting EXP-017 to ~25 markets/game instead of ~175–280. Fix: same `for mkt in player_mkts:` loop as T-115; `X`/`p_est` computed once per player, each threshold evaluated independently. Report adds `markets_logged` field (total forecasts); `players_matched` counts unique players. Print shows `thr=` to distinguish markets per player. Impact: EXP-017 accumulates ~7–8× more data per game; 66-bet APR-0017 floor crossable in <1 Finals game vs. ~3 games. 589/589 tests pass. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[T-115]** Expand run_paper_trade.py to evaluate ALL Kalshi thresholds per player, not just the closest one | `closest = min(player_mkts, ...)` evaluated 1 of ~7–8 available Kalshi thresholds per player — suppressing throughput 7–8×. The fix: compute `X` and `p_est` once per player, then loop over all `player_mkts` entries as independent signals. Each threshold is independently idempotency-checked (per-ticker), independently evaluated by C4, and independently sized. This restores design-intent throughput: ~25–40 bets/game for regular season T1, vs ~3–10 with single-threshold. Critical for G6 evidence accumulation — Finals T1-only single-threshold estimated 70 bets max across 7 games, borderline against the 66-bet floor with no margin. All-threshold behavior gives 175–280 bets across 7 Finals games. 438/438 tests pass. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[ADR-0006]** Delete `max_concurrent_positions` count gate from C4 entirely — no replacement count-based limit | `max_concurrent_positions: 10.0` was a G4 stub that survived three gate clearances (G5/G6/G7) without being validated against the actual architecture. Count is the wrong primitive for a multi-band, 80-player, multi-game quant execution system. The correct risk framework is: pipeline filtering → Kelly sizing → per-event cap ($500) → daily loss limit ($1,000) → drawdown kill switch (20%). If the bot works as designed, thousands of trades per game should flow through the pipeline. A hard count ceiling of 10 is architecturally incoherent and would silently suppress bet accumulation on every multi-game regular season night. See GG-001 in GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[ADR-0007]** Mandate GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md as a required project artifact; extend session-start protocol to 3-step scan + ARE WE COMPROMISED? check | GG-001/002/003 revealed that the governance system correctly documented decisions but had no mechanism to detect when early decisions became wrong as the architecture grew. GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md registers process failures permanently so every future session can see the full failure history. ARE WE COMPROMISED? is a 5-question strategic integrity check added to MEMORY_BANK.md — mandatory at session start, before any implementation work. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | Arb framework: detection band ≠ execution band — architectural separation is load-bearing | Complement lock and line shopping detect opportunities at T1/T2 (expensive computation) and execute at T3/T4 (lookup + fire). Without pre-populated opportunity tables from T1/T2, T3/T4 cannot meet their compute SLAs (500ms and 50ms respectively). T4 requires zero API calls; all data must be pre-staged at T3. This separation is the structural reason arb cannot be treated as a single-band strategy. Documented in ARB_FRAMEWORK.md Section 2 and EXECUTION_BANDS.md arb section. | DS |
| 2026-05-31 | All paper bets placed before ADR-0006 (2026-06-01) invalidated as G6 evidence; portfolio reset to $1,000; G6 counter reset to 0/30 | Bets from the pre-ADR-0006 system are architecturally tainted: `max_concurrent_positions: 10` was silently suppressing bet accumulation. A system with an incoherent count gate is not the system being validated — the evidence it produces cannot be used to certify the corrected system. All prior positions (6 resolved + 3 open Finals G1 stubs) are discarded. Clean G6 evidence begins from NBA Finals G1 (Jun 3, 2026) on the ADR-0006-corrected system. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[T-091]** P&L monitoring alert added to SystemMonitor — percentage-based thresholds, not fixed dollar amounts | `check_portfolio_pnl()` uses 5%/10% of `initial_bankroll` as L1/L2 thresholds rather than $50/$100 absolute values. Rationale: fixed dollar thresholds become meaningless as bankroll changes (after drawdown the same dollar loss represents a larger fraction of capital). Percentage-based thresholds scale correctly at all bankroll levels. L1 → C6 INFO `pnl_alert_l1_watch`; L2 → C6 WARNING `pnl_alert_l2_loss`. Wired into PIPE-03 (run_paper_trade.py) and PIPE-03R (run_resolve.py). DS sign-off: Earl Ignacio 2026-06-01. KI-012 RESOLVED. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | Venue-agnostic VenueParams + ArbOpportunity data model adopted as the centralized arb infrastructure | All arb math (complement lock, stat arb, line shopping) uses injectable VenueParams — no venue-specific constants in strategy logic. ArbOpportunityLog is the unified persistent record across all strategies and venues. This enables: (1) new venues to be added by registering VenueParams without changing strategy code; (2) unified P&L and fill-rate tracking across venue types; (3) opportunity decay analysis. Documented in ARB_FRAMEWORK.md Sections 3–4. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[T-113 / APR-0017 v1 — SUPERSEDED by v2 same session]** G6 evidence standard replaced: count-based APR-0016 → 4-tier multi-dimensional framework | APR-0016 failed Q7 broken-system test (GG-005): 30 bets + positive P&L can be produced by a miscalibrated model on one lucky Finals night with no calibration evidence, no commercial validation, and no execution integrity check. APR-0017 v1 uses sequential tiers — Tier 0 (commercial preconditions: EXP-017 Part B + price discovery), Tier 1 (execution integrity over ≥5 game nights with arbitrary counts T1-A/T1-C), Tier 2 (model performance trends: calibration, EV, Brier, P&L slope), Tier 3 (risk control confirmation). Statistical floor: ≥50 resolved bets + ≥5 game nights. **⚠ v1 contained arbitrary count thresholds in Tier 1 — superseded by v2 below.** T0-B already met (T-114). T0-A (EXP-017 Part B) is the remaining precondition before the paper period counts. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[T-113 / APR-0017 v2 — ACCEPTED]** APR-0017 Tier 1 arbitrary counts replaced with derived rate + slope criteria; statistical floor revised from ≥50 to ≥66; PO/DS sign-off received 2026-06-01 | Operator instruction: "do not use arbitrary gating thresholds, use trend analysis sloping to ensure figures are not arbitrary but specified per tier." v1 contained T1-A ("≥5 distinct game nights") and T1-C ("≥20 confirmed resolutions") — dimensional flaw identical to APR-0016. v2 design principle: every criterion is a rate, slope, or proportion. Where a count floor appears, derivation is shown. Key changes: (1) T1-A → pipeline error rate ≤ 5% (derived: ~1,250× expected API failure rate; tolerant ceiling for non-API system errors) AND slope ≤ 0 (non-increasing); (2) T1-C → resolution fidelity rate ≥ 85% (derived: 100% − 1.5× expected legitimate failure rate) AND slope ≥ 0 (non-decreasing); (3) T1-B (idempotency) + T1-D (audit completeness) kept zero-tolerance with explicit justification that these are boolean structural properties, not rates; (4) Tier 2 slope criteria made explicit: all T2 pass conditions include both a level check and a slope direction. Statistical floor: ≥66 resolved bets (derived from T2-A calibration power at α=0.05, power=0.80, δ=15pp: n = (z_α/2 + z_β)² × p̂q̂ / δ² ≈ 84; relaxed to 66 at α=0.10 accepting moderate power given Finals series constraint); ≥5 game nights (derived from slope regression df ≥ 3). | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[T-114]** Market data (price discovery) always uses Kalshi production API regardless of BOT_ENV; order submission follows BOT_ENV | Paper mode must suppress order submission only. Using demo prices for edge = model_p - market_price produces edge against artificial/seeded prices → meaningless G6 evidence. Fix: `_price_discovery_url()` always returns production URL; `_kalshi_base_url()` follows BOT_ENV for authenticated portfolio and order endpoints. 30 tests added to test_kalshi_client.py, including three URL-routing invariant tests that will permanently catch any regression to the old behavior. | DS |
| 2026-05-29 | Strategy D (stat arb / OU) restricted to T1/T2 bands only; T3/T4/T5 explicitly excluded | OU mean reversion horizon in prediction markets is hours, not seconds. In-play execution (T4/T5) of a spread reversion strategy is not viable — the reversion materially cannot complete within a game window. T3 execution is conditionally valid only when a T1-estimated z-score is still in the entry zone at tip-off. D is therefore a T1/T2-native strategy, unlike C and E which span T3/T4. Binary settlement creates additional OU edge cases not present in equity stat arb — requires dedicated backtesting before activation (Phase 8). | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[ADR-0009 / T-119 / T-120]** Multi-arm architecture Layer 2: shadow model policy accepted; ShadowForecaster built in C3; T-119 model version hash guard live in PIPE-02 | ADR-0004 frozen model policy creates single-component fracture risk: if LR+Platt calibration degrades, Strategies A/B/F/I collapse simultaneously with no warm fallback. Three-layer architecture addresses this: Layer 1 = Type 2 structural edge diversification (E+C — activates with T-077/APR-0010); Layer 2 = shadow model in C3 (this decision); Layer 3 = Strategy I Poisson/NB post-G6. Shadow model: loads `models/shadow_model.joblib` if present; runs parallel inference at PIPE-02 time; never routes to C4; logs to C6 with `shadow=True`; C7 tracks `shadow_brier_30` via `_shadow_window`; promotion fires `shadow_promotion_flag` WARNING when shadow beats prod by ≥ 0.005 Brier over ≥ 30 events. Promotion requires operator review + ADR-0010 + DS sign-off. T-119: PIPE-02 now verifies `artifact["model_version"]` against `prod_model_meta.json` at startup — CRITICAL + exit(1) on mismatch. `shadow_p_estimate` + `shadow_model_version` included in all pregame forecast records for EXP-017 comparison. 612/612 tests pass. | DS |

Without a machine-readable status field, a future code change could silently activate unvalidated strategies. SKELETON is the holding state: code runs, signals are logged, bets do not execute. Promotion to ACTIVE requires EXP/ADR evidence + DECISION_LOG entry. | AO |

ArbBandOrchestrator routes arb signals; MultiBandOrchestrator routes model-edge signals. Risk engine interface differs: arb uses net_profit_usd (risk-neutral); model-edge uses Kelly (directional confidence). | AO |
| 2026-05-29 | **[BAND FRAMEWORK]** Kalshi T3 constraint: max_api_calls_per_decision=1 (single call enforced) | Kalshi API P90 latency estimated at 350ms. T3 execution SLA is 500ms. Headroom: 150ms. Any second live API call at T3 violates the SLA and breaks the momentum scan. The T3 decision tree design (fetch_current_ask → compare_to_cached_T1 → signal) is sound IF single-call is enforced. This must be a code-level constraint, not a convention. VenueBandConstraint for V-001 at T3 sets max_api_calls_per_decision=1. | AO |

| 2026-05-31 | **Kalshi auth OPEN BLOCKER: NOT_FOUND on /portfolio/balance — root cause not resolved** | RSA signing with PKCS1v15 padding → `NOT_FOUND`. RSA signing with PSS padding (PS256) → same `NOT_FOUND`. Bearer token with UUID key ID → `token authentication failure`. All three auth approaches produce errors. Root cause hypothesis: demo API keys visible in Kalshi dashboard (UUID-type) may use session-based auth (POST /log_in → JWT) rather than RSA signing. Alternatively, the RSA public key from `kalshi_bot.pem` was never uploaded/associated with the UUID key ID on Kalshi's side. Registered as active blocker for T-111 (next session). Bot continues in local-only mode (degraded mode by design — no halt). **→ RESOLVED T-111 2026-05-31 — see corrective entry below.** | DS |
| 2026-05-31 | **submit_order body fix: exactly one of yes_price or no_price (not both)** | Kalshi API rejected orders with `invalid_order: exactly one of yes_price, no_price, yes_price_dollars, or no_price_dollars should be provided`. Prior code sent both `yes_price` and `no_price` in every order body. Fix: dict key expression sends only the relevant side's price field. Confirmed: first live demo order executed — Order 363c5f5b, 1 contract YES on KXWTACHALLENGERMATCH-26JUN01SAKCOS-COS at 62¢, status `executed`, balance $999.36, portfolio_value $0.62. Full execution path proven end-to-end. | EL |
| 2026-05-31 | **T-111 RESOLVED: Kalshi auth root cause — wrong base URL (legacy domain doesn't serve authenticated endpoints)** | The code used `api.elections.kalshi.com` as the base URL — the old legacy domain, which serves public market-data endpoints but returns 404 on all authenticated endpoints (portfolio/balance, portfolio/orders). Correct URLs per official Kalshi docs: demo=`external-api.demo.kalshi.co/trade-api/v2`; live=`external-api.kalshi.com/trade-api/v2`. RSA-PSS signing was correct throughout — the signature algorithm, headers, and message construction were all right. The request was simply going to the wrong server. Fix: replaced `KALSHI_BASE_URL` constant with `_kalshi_base_url()` function that reads `BOT_ENV` at runtime (paper→demo, live→live). Balance endpoint response parsing also fixed (API returns flat `{"balance": <cents>, "balance_dollars": "<str>"}`, not nested dict). Confirmed: `Balance: True {'balance_usd': 1000.0, 'portfolio_value_usd': 0.0}`. `cryptography` package added to requirements-base.txt. | EL |
| 2026-05-31 | **summary() redesign: explicit realized_drawdown_pct / deployed_pct split over simple rename** | Old `drawdown_pct` formula (1 − bankroll/initial) showed 31.7% "drawdown" when the system had 0% realized losses — because open stakes (money in flight) were counted as lost capital. Money deployed in open positions is categorically different from permanently lost capital. C4 kill switch was already using the correct formula (realized_loss / initial); only the summary report was wrong. "Best fix" approach chosen: remove `drawdown_pct` entirely, add two explicit fields — `realized_drawdown_pct` (C4 kill switch metric: closed losses / initial) and `deployed_pct` (liquidity metric: open stakes / initial). All three callers updated; tests updated to assert both new fields and reject old field. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **EXP-017 Part A confirms: model edge is positive across market prices 0.40–0.60 (T-105)** | Sensitivity sweep ran on 2,692 TEST rows (2025-26 season). EV is positive at ALL swept market price levels — including at MIP=0.40, 0.43, ..., 0.60. EV at null (0.50) = +0.0541/bet. EV *increases* as market prices move away from 0.50: at MIP=0.60, EV = +0.1171/bet (NO bets dominate — model correctly identifies YES is overpriced when market prices at 0.60 but actual positive rate is 47%). Interpretation: unless Kalshi is pricing within ~0.02 of the model's estimate (extremely efficient), model finds positive EV. This is strong directional evidence but does NOT close GG-004 — Part A uses hypothetical uniform prices. Part B with actual Kalshi prices is required for definitive confirmation. run_exp017.py created; operator action required for Part B data collection. | DS |
| 2026-05-31 | **T2 activation gate corrected: FEATURE_VERSION 1.3.0 removed; replaced with EXP validation requirement (T-106)** | EXECUTION_BANDS.md line 190 referenced `FEATURE_VERSION 1.3.0` as a T2 activation precondition. EXP-012 falsified the injury signal at current data scale (HYP-006 falsified; version stays 1.2.0 permanently unless a new experiment validates the injury feature set at a larger N). FEATURE_VERSION 1.3.0 is not a real target — it was a placeholder for "injury features work, build them." Since they don't work at current N, the gate is correctly: DS-04 operational + dedicated EXP confirming Brier improvement ≥ 0.001 at N ≥ 100. This aligns EXECUTION_BANDS.md with BAND_ASSIGNMENT_FRAMEWORK.md which already documented this correctly. DS sign-off confirming the intent is preserved. | DS |
| 2026-05-31 | **APR-0016 "30 resolved bets" G6 gate flagged for structural rework — bet count is the wrong primary metric; multi-dimensional evidence standard proposed** | APR-0016 answers "did you place 30 bets?" It does not answer "does the system work?" Three structural failures: (1) Count is meaningless at Finals throughput — 30 bets can accumulate in one game night; one night's result is variance-dominated, not signal. (2) Count is blind to calibration — 30 lucky bets on a miscalibrated model passes. (3) Count is blind to commercial validity — 30 bets at demo artificial prices against fake prices passes without proving real edge. Proposed replacement: **sequential evidence standard with four tiers, each a precondition for the next.** Tier 0 (Preconditions, must pass before evidence counting begins): EXP-017 Part B confirmed (real Kalshi prices show positive EV); price discovery confirmed using production API prices. Tier 1 (Execution Integrity): pipeline fires correctly on ≥5 distinct game nights; zero duplicate bets (idempotency confirmed live); ≥20 resolved positions confirmed correct against nba_api; audit log clean. Tier 2 (Model Performance Trends): realized win rate within ±15% of predicted probability; realized EV (P&L / wagered) positive; Brier score on paper bets ≤ 0.265 (within L1 drift threshold); **P&L slope trending positive over game nights** — not just net positive at sample end. Tier 3 (Risk Control): no bet exceeds per-event cap; drawdown never exceeds 15% during paper period; C4 rejection rate 20–80% (selectivity confirmed). Statistical floor: ≥50 resolved bets provides meaningful power; this is a floor for validity, not the gate itself. A bet count that passes the floor while failing any trend criterion is an automatic gate failure. A superseding APR (APR-XXXX) is required before this framework is operative. APR-0016 remains the active gate until superseded. T-113 registered. | DS |

| 2026-06-01 | **[DESIGN THROUGHPUT INVARIANT] Bot design intent is hundreds to thousands of bets per game night — 66-bet G6 floor is non-binding; BINDING G6 constraints are EXP-017 Part B + ≥5 game nights** | Corrective decision logged after repeated context erosion across 20+ sessions. Full operation (T1+T2+T3+T4 active, regular season 10+ simultaneous games, 400+ markets/night, multi-venue) produces 100–1,000+ bets per night under T1 alone; thousands at full scale. The 66-bet statistical floor is derived from calibration power and is trivially exceeded in 1-2 regular season nights — it is NOT a meaningful accumulation challenge. The Finals series (~38 markets, T1-only, 1 game/night) is a structural constraint of the current paper window, not a representation of design throughput. The ACTUAL binding G6 constraints are: (1) GG-004 resolved via EXP-017 Part B (positive EV vs. real Kalshi prices confirmed); (2) ≥5 game nights for slope regression degrees of freedom. If both are met in the first week of 2026-27 regular season, G6 can close that week. The dark period (2026-06 to 2026-10) is for: T2/T3 production-ready, model retrain before October 2026, EXP-017 Part B confirmation — NOT for "slowly accumulating bets." Added as invariant #11 in MEMORY_BANK.md, clarifying note in APR-0017 statistical floor section, and design throughput section in CLAUDE.md. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[THROUGHPUT ANALYSIS] Optimum bets per NBA game quantified from first principles; KI-022 registered** | Direct code + market data analysis. Cohort: 75 players (v1:50, v2:22, v3:3). Kalshi market depth: ~7–8 threshold markets per player per game (38 total / 5 matched players in Finals pregame_20260531.json). Critical finding: `run_paper_trade.py` line 290 evaluates ONE threshold per player (closest to pts_l10_mean), suppressing ~7–8× available throughput. Build-up table by design layer (regular season game): (1) T1-only, KXNBAPTS, 1 threshold/player = 3–10 bets; (2) T1-only, KXNBAPTS, all thresholds = 25–40 bets; (3) T1+T2+T3, KXNBAPTS, all thresholds = 45–75 bets; (4) T1+T2+T3+T4, KXNBAPTS, all thresholds = 80–120 bets; (5) T1–T4, multi-stat (pts+reb+ast+3PT), all thresholds = 300–500 bets; (6) T1–T4, multi-stat, multi-venue (Kalshi+Polymarket) = 500–900 bets. Regular season night (15 games, full design): 7,500–13,500 bets. Design-intent floor of 200 bets per game is achievable at T1+T2+T3 + multi-stat + all thresholds — no T4, no Polymarket required. The 66-bet G6 floor is crossed at T1-only + all thresholds + regular season on a single game night. KI-022 registered: single-threshold selection is the first multiplier to unlock; estimated ~15-line code change; must resolve before 2026-27 regular season. T-115 designated. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[KI-021] sklearn version mismatch (trained 1.4.2 / inference 1.8.0) investigated and cleared** | Direct probability comparison: 205 test vectors (200 random + 5 boundary) scored locally (1.4.2) and on VPS (1.8.0) using the same serialized LogisticRegression+Platt pipeline. max_abs_delta = 0.00106 (< 0.1% probability point); median_abs_delta = 0.00022; zero cases where \|delta\| > 0.02 (MIN_EDGE); zero bet direction flips at realistic mid-prices (0.45–0.60). Version gap produces sub-0.1% numerical noise, not calibration drift. ECE 0.0342 guarantee from ADR-0004 is not materially affected. KI-021 registered at LOW severity; resolution at T-072 (pin scikit-learn==1.4.2 in VPS venv at model refresh). KI-020 (Kalshi auth) also marked RESOLVED — T-111/T-112 complete 2026-05-31. | DS |

| 2026-06-01 | **[GG-006 / KI-023 / T-116 — Ungated risk registration from PDTP analysis]** Three risks identified as ungated in Session 8 PDTP analysis; formally registered in governance registers same session | PDTP Pass 3 synthesis and adversarial scan identified three HIGH-severity risks not captured in any governance document: (1) **GG-006**: Kalshi TOS review never conducted for automated/algorithmic trading — risk that bot usage violates Kalshi operational terms. | 2026-06-01 | **[GG-006 SUBSTANTIALLY RESOLVED — Kalshi DCM v1.26 + DCO v1.4 rulebooks read in full]** Position accountability framework fully understood; per-contract Terms and Conditions are the authoritative source for numerical limits; fully-collateralized structure provides economic binding constraint at current bankroll | Full rulebook review (Session 9). Key findings: (1) **PAL definition**: "Position Accountability Level" = dollar-loss threshold above which Kalshi may require disclosure/reduction — SOFT, not a ban. "Position Limit" = maximum dollar-loss allowed — HARD cap. Both defined in dollar-loss terms (maximum loss on position, not contract count or notional). (2) **Rule 5.18 (Position Accountability)**: PALs exist for all contracts; each PAL specified in the contract's individual Terms and Conditions on Kalshi website — NOT in main DCM Rulebook body. Exceeding PAL → mandatory disclosure to Kalshi Compliance + potential reduction if instructed. (3) **Rule 5.19 (Position Limits)**: Hard caps per contract; also specified in per-contract Terms and Conditions. Rule 5.19(h): "In no instance may a Trader's position exceed any applicable limit established by the CFTC." First violation → warning letter; subsequent violations within 12 months → formal proceedings. (4) **Rule 5.4(a)(a)/(d)**: Exchange-level order rate limiter explicitly anticipated by rulebook; automated order submissions are standard operating mode; automated participants must maintain "adequate controls to prevent excessive messaging" — our rate-limiting architecture satisfies this. (5) **Market Maker exemptions**: 10× PAL + Position Limit exemptions for Market Maker obligation contracts. Earl is NOT a Market Maker. (6) **DCO Rulebook v1.4 Rule 7.5 Fully Collateralized Positions**: Clearing house (Kalshi Klear LLC) will not clear positions exceeding deposited funds. This is the binding structural constraint: Earl's $683 balance IS the effective position ceiling. Kalshi cannot accept positions that exceed deposited collateral — regulatory PAL is irrelevant until bankroll grows well beyond current paper trading scale. (7) **CRITICAL ARCHITECTURAL FINDING**: The DCM Rulebook 1.26 main body contains ZERO numerical PAL or Position Limit values for any contract. The actual limits are in each contract's individual Terms and Conditions on Kalshi's website. Any prior assumption that these limits could be verified from the main rulebook PDF was incorrect. **Remaining GG-006 item**: check KXNBAPTS contract-specific Terms page on Kalshi for exact PAL/Position Limit numbers — required before G6 ADR-0005 but LOW risk at current bankroll ($10–80/bet, $683 balance). Practical resolution path: log into Kalshi, open any active KXNBAPTS contract, read the Terms and Conditions tab. GG-006 updated to SUBSTANTIALLY RESOLVED in GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md and MEMORY_BANK.md. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[SESSION 12] StagedOrderStore (SQLite, append-only, WAL mode) adopted as the pre-staging handoff layer between T1/T2 computation and T4 in-play execution** | The "detection band ≠ execution band" principle (logged 2026-05-29) is now instantiated as a concrete component: `StagedOrderStore` in `c4_risk_engine/staged_order_store.py`. T1/T2 computes C4-approved orders (Kelly-sized, direction set) and stages them with a `valid_until` expiry. T4 fires from the staged lookup with zero API calls at execution time — Bayesian inference is the fallback path when no pre-staged order exists. Statuses: STAGED → FIRED or EXPIRED or CANCELLED. No DELETEs — append-only audit trail. SQLite WAL mode for concurrent read safety. Default db_path: `logs/staged_orders.db`. This is Phase 7 dark-period infrastructure; T4 live wiring (PIPE-07/08) is blocked on three Phase 8 ADRs: DS-02 source selection, kill switch redesign (NFR-03 60s → <5s), and Phase 8 infrastructure ADR. All three require operator architectural decision. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[SESSION 12] T4_MIN_DELTA_P = 0.08 adopted as T4 Bayesian materiality threshold** | New threshold: minimum absolute change in probability (|p_t4 − p_t1|) required to treat a T4 Bayesian signal as material. Calibrated conservatively: (1) Kelly fraction at T4 is 0.05 (lowest active band); small delta-p at 0.05 Kelly produces near-zero position sizes after vig deduction; (2) MIN_EDGE = 0.02 — a delta-p below 0.08 in a regime where vig is 0.04–0.06 implies p_t4 may not reliably exceed mip + vig across the signal's uncertainty; (3) 0.08 ≈ 4× MIN_EDGE, providing a margin of signal strength commensurate with T4's reduced Kelly confidence. Defined in `c3_forecasting/game_state_model.py:T4_MIN_DELTA_P`. Must be calibrated against real in-play data before T4 ACTIVE designation — this value is a Phase 7 skeleton constant, not a validated production threshold. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[SESSION 12] Poisson CDF (pure Python stdlib) adopted for T4 Bayesian engine and T5 late-game arithmetic; no scipy/numpy dependency in model layer** | Both T4 and T5 use `P(X ≥ k_needed | λ) = 1 − CDF(k_needed − 1, λ)` where λ = pace_pts_per_min × time_remaining_min and k_needed = ceil(threshold − pts_so_far). Pure Python iterative CDF (math.exp, no external libraries): loop runs ≤ k_needed+1 iterations. NBA scores are integers — ceiling is required to handle .5-point Kalshi thresholds correctly. T4 budget: 50ms (1,000 calls in <1ms verified in test). T5 budget: 5ms (1,000 calls in <0.5ms verified in test). Rationale: keeping inference in stdlib removes scipy/sklearn from the T4 execution path — critical for kill-switch redesign flexibility when the execution hot-loop is redesigned for <5s NFR-03 compliance at Phase 8. scipy dependency in C3 is correct for model training; it must not appear in the inference path. | DS |
| 2026-06-01 | **[SESSION 12] Phase 7/8/9 T4 iteration sequencing adopted as dark-period roadmap** | Operator approved the following build order after reviewing the strategic plan: **Phase 7 (dark period, now):** `game_state_model.py` (Poisson math) + `staged_order_store.py` (pre-staging handoff) + `run_t4_inplay.py` skeleton (unified T4 decision tree with FIRE_STAGED / FIRE_BAYESIAN / FIRE_T5 / NO_ACTION routing). No DS-02 required — all testable on synthetic data. **Phase 8 (blocked on 3 operator ADRs):** (1) DS-02 source ADR — real-time game state feed selection (ESPN feed, SportsData.io, or other; latency + reliability + cost tradeoffs are Earl's call); (2) Kill switch redesign ADR — NFR-03 60s → <5s T4/T5 redesign (C7 WebSocket interrupt vs. shared memory flag vs. hardware kill); (3) Phase 8 infrastructure ADR — T4/T5 activation gate (VPS hardware, process isolation, monitoring). **Phase 8+:** Strategy D stat arb, J momentum, K in-play scalp — all require dedicated EXPs before build. This sequencing is the authoritative dark-period roadmap. No implementation beyond Phase 7 scope is authorized without the three Phase 8 ADRs signed. | DS |

| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 14] ADR-0010 accepted: Band Kelly hierarchy revision + all thresholds validated numerically** | ADR-0010 formalizes band-specific Kelly fractions from Poisson signal uncertainty model. Variance-scaling principle: k_band = k_ref × (σ_λ_ref / σ_λ_band). T1 σ_λ = 2.055 (pts_l10_mean, 10-game estimate); derived k = 0.049, 2× operational premium → 0.10. T4 σ_λ ≈ 0.5 in Q3/Q4 firing window; derived k = 0.19, rounded → 0.20. T5 is near-deterministic arithmetic (p≥0.85 gate); quarter-Kelly on f* → 0.25. T4_MIN_DELTA_P = 0.08 retained for paper prototype: 2σ noise floor at min 44+ = 0.069 (0.08 exceeds floor), economic floor = MIN_EDGE + VIG_HALF = 0.045; binding constraint shifts from economic to statistical as the game progresses; 0.08 is phase-appropriate for late Q4 and generates sufficient EXP-018 calibration data. T5_YES_CERTAINTY = 0.85 and T5_NO_CERTAINTY = 0.15 are grounded: at ≤3 min, λ_remaining ≈ 1.25 pts; P(score ≥ 1 more | λ=1.25) = 0.713; P(score ≥ 3 more) = 0.131 — the 0.85/0.15 gate correctly filters outcomes where ≥3 pts still needed. Economic floor for T5: p_min = market + MIN_EDGE + VIG_HALF = 0.845 at market=0.80; 0.85 exceeds floor. Code changes authorized by ADR-0010: execution_bands.py (T1→0.10, T2→0.13, T3→0.07, T4→0.20, T5→0.25), game_state_model.py (KELLY_T4=0.20, KELLY_T5=0.25), risk.py (RESERVE_MANDATE_PCT=0.40, warning-only at paper scale). | DS |

| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 13] p_t4 clarified as Poisson current-state model (NOT Bayesian posterior); p_t1 is materiality benchmark only — docstrings corrected in game_state_model.py and run_t4_inplay.py** | Docstrings in `game_state_model.py` and `run_t4_inplay.py` used "Bayesian update" and "T1 prior" language that contradicted the code. The code computes p_t4 entirely from current game state (pts_so_far, pace, time_remaining) via Poisson CDF — p_t1 is never incorporated into the probability computation. Correct framing: (1) p_t4 is a fresh Poisson estimate from current game state; (2) p_t1 serves only two roles — the materiality gate (|p_t4 − p_t1| ≥ T4_MIN_DELTA_P required to act) and the fire condition reference (p_t4 > mip + vig). Design rationale: by T4 time, current game state is the dominant sufficient statistic; incorporating p_t1 into the Poisson calculation would introduce pre-game bias into in-game inference and contradict this principle. The "Bayesian" label was architectural baggage from early T4 design iteration, not a description of what the code does. Four corrections made: `game_state_model.py` module docstring (removed "Bayesian update / T1 prior" framing), `GameStateModel` class docstring (same), `T4Signal.p_t1` field comment ("benchmark" not "prior"), `T4Signal.p_t4` field comment ("fresh from game state; not a posterior"); and `run_t4_inplay.py` `GameStateEvent.p_t1` field docstring (clarified two-role usage). No algorithmic change — math was always correct. Consequence: KI-024 (stale `pace_pts_per_min`) registered as the dominant T4/T5 model risk; GG-007 (T4_MIN_DELTA_P + T5 certainty threshold calibration gap) registered; T-128 / EXP-018 / EXP-019 queued for dark period. | DS |

| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 17] Market-first loop replacing cohort-first in PIPE-02 + PIPE-03** | Root cause of ~24-bet ceiling: PIPE-02/PIPE-03 iterated over the 55-player EXP-005 cohort and found matching Kalshi markets. Fix: invert loop — iterate over all active Kalshi markets with live quotes, then resolve player stats via cohort lookup or dynamic fallback. `cohort_lookup: dict[str, pd.Series]` built once from normalized player names. `markets_by_player: dict[str, list]` groups Kalshi markets by player. No architectural change to C4 or C6 — only the order pipeline constructs candidates. Impact: any player with a live Kalshi market is now evaluated, not just the 55-player cohort. Finals context clarification logged: realistic maximum for 1-game/night Finals is ~50 bets (Kalshi KXNBAPTS universe bottleneck, not bot architecture); 1000s-of-bets claim applies to regular season (8+ games, 2400+ markets). | DS |

| 2026-06-02 | **[SESSION 17] Batch reserve gate added to PIPE-03 (RESERVE_MANDATE_PCT = 0.40)** | Root cause: Jun 1 incident — single PIPE-03 run deployed $911 of $1,000 bankroll (91.2%) in one batch. Gate is at the execution layer (PIPE-03), not C4 — C4 still evaluates all forecasts independently. `_initial_bankroll` is the value at PIPE-03 startup (not historical). Multi-run protection is the existing `RESERVE_MANDATE_PCT` warning in `evaluate_forecast()` (C4 layer). | DS |

| 2026-06-03 | **[SESSION 22] MIN_BET_T1_PAPER=1.0 wired into run_paper_trade.py; $10 absolute floor is capital-ceiling-specific, not capital-ceiling-agnostic** | Root cause: `MIN_BET_USD = 10.0` in `risk.py _HARD_LIMITS` was calibrated for live trading (Kalshi practical minimums, proportional execution overhead). At $1,000 paper bankroll with T1_KELLY=0.10, Kelly × bankroll on low-f* markets produces bet sizes of $1.55–$9.83 — rejected by the $10 floor. 8 of 35 T1 markets were silently suppressed per session, reducing throughput from 35 to ~24. This is architecturally incorrect: the design intent is capital-ceiling-agnostic execution where bet count scales consistently regardless of whether bankroll is $500, $1,000, or $1M. At $1M bankroll, the same bets produce $1,550–$9,830 (floor non-binding). The $10 floor is a live-capital operational constant, not a structural architectural property. Fix: `MIN_BET_T1_PAPER = float(os.environ.get("MIN_BET_T1_PAPER", "1.0"))` added to `run_paper_trade.py` (mirrors `MIN_BET_T4_PAPER=1.0` pattern in `run_t4_live.py` SESSION 18). Both `RiskEngine` instantiations in PIPE-03 (lines 242, 439) now receive `min_bet_usd=MIN_BET_T1_PAPER`. `run_monte_carlo.py` updated: `DEFAULT_MIN_BET` reads from `MIN_BET_T1_PAPER` env var; `--min-bet` CLI arg added; `min_bet` threaded through `build_report()`. Paper default for both files: $1.0. Live `MIN_BET_USD=10.0` in `_HARD_LIMITS` unchanged — no ADR required (paper path only). Architectural note: percentage-based minimum (e.g. `max(1.0, bankroll × 0.001)`) should replace the absolute floor at G6 when live bankroll is set, via ADR. 695/695 tests pass. Effect: all 35 T1 markets fire per window (was 24); both PIPE-03 windows produce ~35 bets each; T4 VIRTUAL contributes ~1,050 evaluation events per game. Total evaluation events for Finals G1: ~1,120, meeting the ~1,000/game design throughput floor (CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9). | DS |

The architectural requirement is: the system must HANDLE ~1,000 bets firing (capital sizing, kill switch, execution pipeline all correct at that scale). Not that ~1,000 bets must fire. Rationale: (1) LR model does not drive bets (architecture finding above); (2) EXP-012 showed F-040 adds nothing to LR Brier (delta = −7e-6); (3) Fox 25+ root cause from EXP-017 Part B is Poisson λ having no opponent context — λ=27.5 (raw avg) when Finals Knicks defense should suppress it; (4) Poisson CDF is the exact mechanism that generated the DIRECTIONAL_NEGATIVE result. The redesign targets the actual bug. No LR retrain required. `LEAGUE_AVG_PTS_ALLOWED = 113.0` (2025-26 NBA season average); update at season rollover via DECISION_LOG entry (no new ADR required). Clip bounds (0.70, 1.30) prevent extreme adjustments from teams with <10 games in rolling window. | DS |

Rationale: δ=15pp detection threshold was set for the baseline T1-only model. As model accuracy improves (smaller calibration error, tighter confidence intervals), the required sample size for the same statistical power decreases. Conversely, as more signals are added, the effective model complexity increases and the floor may need adjustment upward. The ≥66 floor is NOT frozen — it is derived from a formula whose inputs change as the model evolves. Action required: at each major model version change (new feature set, retrain, new ADR), re-derive the floor from the calibration power formula with updated δ estimate. | 2026-06-04 | **[SESSION 31 / T-133] fee_contaminated false-positive bug fixed in paper_trader.py; EXP-018 contamination concern ruled out** | Q6 adversarial check (Concern 2) confirmed: 5 of 9 Finals G2 T1 paper bets had `fee_contaminated=True` despite being placed post-ADR-0016 with valid positive directional edge. Root cause: `fee_net_edge = gross_edge - fee_per_contract` where `gross_edge` is signed YES-direction. For NO-direction bets, `gross_edge` is negative by definition → `fee_net_edge` is always negative → `fee_contaminated` always True for NO bets, regardless of actual directional edge. Fix: `_directional_gross = gross_edge if direction == "YES" else -gross_edge`; `fee_contaminated = bool(_directional_gross - fee_per_contract < 0.0)`. This is a labeling bug only — the bets themselves were never bad. The 5 NO bets (Wembanyama NO 35/30/25, Castle NO 25, Vassell NO 25) have genuine positive directional edge (+0.151, +0.172, +0.061, +0.024, +0.056). EXP-018 analyst note: G2 positions with `fee_contaminated=True` and `direction=NO` should be treated as clean (NOT excluded from EXP-018 evidence pool). The `fee_contaminated` field is directionally-correct only for bets placed after commit cd029f2. 715/715 tests pass. Deployed to VPS via git pull (stash-and-drop of identical ADR-0015/ADR-0016 code SESSION 30 had manually pushed). Q6 Concern 1 (adj_λ clip bounds) ruled out: `clip(0.70, 1.30)` = 79–148 pts/game; no NBA Finals team operates near these bounds; clips are data-corruption safety rails, never triggered in practice. Q6 Concern 3 (CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR): env var already in .zshrc line 3; ENOSPC from in-memory task pipe, not disk; find+xargs workaround is the correct permanent mitigation for Step 1 governance scan. | DS |
| 2026-06-04 | **[SESSION 29 / PDTP] T4 IDEMPOTENCY interference pattern identified as structural T1 late-cron suppressor** | Formal PDTP analysis of Finals G1 (PDTP_FINALS_G1_20260604.md) identified that T4 in-game execution pre-empts T1 late cron. T4 fires from tip-off (00:30 UTC) and bets on all 32 tickers within the first hour. The late T1 PIPE-03 cron at 01:30 UTC finds 30+ of 32 tickers IDEMPOTENCY-locked (per-ticker + game_date uniqueness check in `paper_trader.py:open_position()`), leaving only 2–3 gap tickers for new T1 bets. This collapses late-window T1 from MC-predicted ~33 bets to ~3 actual bets. Not a failure — per-ticker idempotency is correct (must not double-bet the same player-threshold). Effect: T1 early cron captures most Kelly-sized bets (3–6 large positions); T4 covers remaining tickers at flat-contract scale; late T1 cron fills only gaps T4 has not yet reached. Implication for G2+: this pattern will repeat every Finals game — T1 early cron is the de facto T1 execution window. Late cron is a gap-fill mechanism, not a parallel window. Architectural note: separating T1 and T4 ticker namespaces (different ticker key structure) would eliminate the interference — but requires ADR and is low-priority while paper T4 is flat-contract. Note for T4 ACTIVE ADR. | DS |
| 2026-06-03 | **[SESSION 25] Fee annotation + injury signal fields added to every paper position record; G6 evidence ledger made self-annotating** | GG-009 contamination risk: Kalshi taker fee `0.07×P×(1−P)` is absent from the MIN_EDGE gate. At P=0.50, fee=$0.0175 = 87.5% of MIN_EDGE=0.02 — bets with gross edge 0.02–0.0375 are fee-negative. KI-011 injury blindness risk: model generates largest apparent-edge signals on injured/questionable players (market ~0.08, model sees historical ~0.55). Without per-position annotation, G6 analysis would require retroactive join to C6 audit logs and fee formula re-application against historical prices. Fix: six new fields added to every `paper_portfolio.json` position record via `paper_trader.py:open_position()`: `model_p` (forecast probability), `gross_edge` (C3 edge before fees), `fee_per_contract` (= `0.07 × mid_price × (1−mid_price)`), `fee_net_edge` (gross_edge − fee_per_contract), `fee_contaminated` (bool: True when fee_net_edge < 0), `probable_injury_signal` (bool: True when `model_p > 0 AND abs(model_p − mid_price) > 0.25`). Calling scripts `run_paper_trade.py` and `run_t4_live.py` enrich the approval dict with `model_p` and `edge` before passing to `open_position()` (C4's `evaluate_forecast()` return dict does not carry these — design constraint preserved). Backward-compatible: `approval.get("model_p", 0.0)` returns 0.0 when not present; existing tests unaffected. At G6: fee-clean subgroup = `[p for p in closed if not p["fee_contaminated"]]`; injury-clean = `[p for p in closed if not p["probable_injury_signal"]]`. Both filters runnable directly from ledger, no retroactive join required. 695/695 tests pass. Commit `8321900`. | DS |

GG-014 registered. T-136 registered. Immediate mitigation options: (1) add `time.sleep(0.5)` between order placements in the execution loop; (2) implement retry with exponential backoff (max 3 retries, 1s base). No code change deployed this session — paper mode masks the severity. Decision deferred to T-136. | DS |

| 2026-06-08 | **[SESSION 39] `scripts/live_terminal.py` — blocking SSH crash fix, scroll latency fix, T4 portfolio fetch, drawdown correction** | Four bug fixes to the live monitoring terminal. No architectural change. (1) **Blocking SSH fetch → background thread (primary crash cause)**: The main curses loop called `fetch_remote()` (SSH subprocess) synchronously. Any SSH delay >1s (VPS load, network hiccup) stalled the entire render/input loop, causing apparent freezes and event queue buildup. Fix: introduced `_FetchState` dataclass + `_fetch_worker()` daemon thread. Main loop reads from `_FetchState.data` under lock; panel content rebuilds on each new data arrival (compare `last_ok` timestamp). SSH latency now completely decoupled from UI responsiveness. Background thread starts at `_curses_loop` entry; `stop_event.set()` on q/Ctrl-C for clean shutdown. (2) **Scroll latency fix**: Two causes. First: draw ran *before* input — scroll changes weren't visible until the next frame (50ms+ later). Second: single `getch()` per loop — OS key-repeat events queued faster than the loop processed them, causing "stuttery" held-key scrolling. Fix: moved input processing *before* the draw, replaced single `getch()` with a drain loop (processes all queued keys per iteration), reduced `time.sleep(0.05)` → `time.sleep(0.016)` (~60fps). Effective scroll latency: sleep (≤16ms) + draw (~10ms) = ~26ms max. Held arrow key now tracks OS key-repeat rate directly. (3) **T4 portfolio fetch**: `paper_portfolio_t4.json` was not fetched — the remote SSH script and `_data_from_local()` only read `paper_portfolio.json`. Added `t4_portfolio` key to both. No visual change; data is available for future panel use and correctness of any T4 display. t4_tail increased 10 → 25 lines for T4 burst visibility. (4) **Drawdown calculation**: `dd_pct` was `(initial - bankroll) / initial`. Because `bankroll_usd` in `paper_portfolio.json` is deducted at `open_position()` and credited back at resolution, deployed stakes appear as a loss. Correct formula: `max(0, -realized_pnl / initial)` — uses `total_realized_pnl` (cash-settled P&L only, ignoring in-flight stakes). Was showing 4.1% drawdown when actual realized P&L is +$75.67. Now shows 0.0%. Commits: `fd4cea5` (threading + T4 portfolio + drawdown), `07210a0` (scroll fix). Both deployed to VPS. | DS |

G2 resolved losses ($130.08) loaded from portfolio at startup + NO bet stakes during run ($43.03) = $173.11. This is correct semantics (loss settled today) but may be surprising. ADR or doc note warranted at G6 limit revision. Zero G3 pre-game bets fired due to this bug — G6 evidence count impact: 0 additional bets from G3 T1 window at 22:00 UTC. 783/783 tests pass after fix. T4 was correctly polling ESPN game 401859965 (G3) with STATUS_SCHEDULED — only the monitor was blind. (3) **T4 main loop crash guard**: Wrapped `run_t4_live.py` main `while True` body in try/except to prevent process crash on Kalshi/ESPN network errors. Kill switch check stays OUTSIDE guard to remain unbreakable. 783/783 tests pass. All fixes committed and deployed to VPS before G3 tip. | DS |
| 2026-06-09 | **[SESSION 40] ADR-0018 ACCEPTED: Band exit mechanics — symmetric filter-out for all execution bands (APR-0018)** | Operator mandate (Earl Ignacio, 2026-06-09 SESSION 40): "Each bucket not only has a filter into — but should have a filter out of, all using quantifiable strategies (with qual enhancers)." Trigger: G3 analysis revealed that T4 (and all current bands) have filter-IN logic (Poisson CDF entry signal, Kelly sizing, MIN_EDGE gate) but NO filter-OUT logic. When `p_t4` drops below `p_t1 - EXIT_THRESHOLD` and an open YES position exists, the system holds with no mechanism to exit — even when the same Bayesian math that fired the entry now argues against the position. Prediction markets are bilateral instruments: SELL YES and BUY NO are both valid exit mechanisms (Kalshi supports both). The system is not exploiting this. Architectural scope of filter-out: (1) **Exit trigger**: `p_t4 < p_t1 - EXIT_THRESHOLD` AND open YES position exists → evaluate exit; symmetric for NO positions. (2) **Exit instrument**: SELL YES or BUY NO (whichever is more favorable given current bid/ask). (3) **Exit sizing**: Kelly inverse — size the exit proportionally to the magnitude of the signal reversal. (4) **Qual enhancer**: T3 FADE signal amplifies exit decision (T3 FADE + Bayesian reversal = stronger exit case than either alone). (5) **Time gate**: no exits in final N minutes when bid/ask spreads widen and market is illiquid. (6) **Cost model**: only exit if E[loss from holding] > spread cost of exit — prevents churning on noise. Implementation targets: `paper_trader.py` (`exit_position()` method), `VenueAdapter` sell order type, `run_t4_live.py` exit path in `_run_cycle()`. NO changes to current open positions or anything deployed. ADR-0018 (Band Exit Mechanics) required before implementation. T-145 registered. | DS |
| 2026-06-09 | **[SESSION 40 / EXP-019A] NOISE_BUFFER_BAND analytic derivation complete: candidate values produced per ADR-0018 Section 2 methodology** | `NOISE_BUFFER_BAND = max(2σ_p, EXIT_NET_COST_FLOOR)` computed at decision boundary `k = ⌊λ⌋+1` (maximum PMF — worst-case noise scenario). σ_p = P(X=k-1\|Poisson(λ)) × σ_λ. σ_λ per band: T4=0.500 (established ADR-0010), T5=0.500×√(2/18)=0.1667 (temporal scaling from T4), T1/T2/T3=1.024 (geometric mean of T4-extrapolated upper bound 0.645 and pre-game prior lower bound 1.625 from ADR-0010's σ_λ=2.055). EXIT_NET_COST_FLOOR=0.0175 (taker fee at mip=0.50, worst case; spread excluded pending EXP-019C). Results at representative player (22 pts/game, adj_pace=0.458 pts/min): T1=0.22053 (statistical), T2=0.22053 (statistical), T3=0.22053 (statistical), T4=0.13905 (statistical), T5=0.13328 (statistical). All bands: statistical noise floor (2σ_p) dominates taker fee floor (0.0175) on every band — exit bars are noise-driven not cost-driven. Key implication: T1/T2/T3 buffer (0.22) is wide because σ_λ=1.024 at Q2 mid is large — the Poisson estimate is noisy when the game is half-complete. Exit fires only on a clear reversal. T4 buffer (0.139) vs T4_MIN_DELTA_P=0.08 (entry): entry bar is lower than exit bar — this is correct; entry requires edge over pre-game estimate; exit requires market to clearly overprice the outcome. T5 buffer (0.133) similar to T4: near-zero λ drives high PMF at k=1 (P(X=0)=exp(-λ)≈0.400), but low σ_λ=0.167 offsets it. Script: `scripts/run_exp019a_analytic.py`. All values are candidate values pending EXP-019A empirical validation (observed p_current variance in paper trajectories). EXP-019C spread data will push final values higher. T1/T2/T3 σ_λ values require empirical validation — geometric mean is a bounded estimate, not a measured posterior. | DS |
| 2026-06-09 | **[SESSION 42 / T-149 / T-150] T4 fee gate fix deployed + aggregate fee break-even analysis (GG-019 Part A + Part B resolution)** | **T-149 (code fix):** `run_t4_live.py` now subtracts Kalshi taker fee before C4 gate, matching T1 behavior per ADR-0016. Change: after edge computation in `_run_cycle`, added `gross_edge = edge; kalshi_fee = 0.07 * mip * (1.0 - mip); net_edge = gross_edge - kalshi_fee`. `fc_record["edge"]` = net_edge (C4 gates on this); `fc_record["gross_edge"]` = gross_edge (paper_trader annotation). `approval` dict now carries both keys. `T4_EXPECTED_TOTAL_EDGE = 441.38` comment annotated as gross (pre-T-149); net ≈ 416 at G1 throughput, needs recalibration after G4. 10 new tests (`tests/test_t149_t4_fee_gate.py`): 8 formula-level regression guards + 2 mock-based integration tests asserting `fc_record["edge"]` is net-of-fee and approval dict carries `gross_edge`. 793/793 tests pass. **T-150 (break-even analysis):** Computed empirically from G2+G3 paper portfolio data. *T4 (G3, 2026-06-08):* 1,115 closed positions; avg mip=0.4484; avg fee/contract=$0.01281; total fee drag=$14.28/game. Fee as % of gross EV: 4.4%. *T1 (G2+G3 avg):* 82 closed positions over 2 games; avg mip=0.407; avg contracts/bet=45.54 (Kelly sizing at $876 paper bankroll); avg fee/contract=$0.01165; G2 total=$7.89, G3 total=$17.88, avg=$12.89/game. Fee as % of directional gross EV: 8.2%. *Combined per Finals game: ~$27.17* (T4 $14.28 + T1 $12.89). *Regular season projection (10 games/night × 82 nights):* T4 ≈ $11,713/season (flat-sized, bankroll-independent); T1 scales with bankroll — at paper scale ($876) projects to $10,567/season; at live capital bankroll, T1 fees grow proportionally. *Break-even condition per contract:* min net_edge > 0.07×mip×(1-mip). At mip=0.50: >0.0175; at avg mip=0.45: >0.0173. `MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE=0.02` is above the taker fee at all observed mip values — **C4 always enforces positive net edge post-T-149.** *ADR-0005 implication:* At paper bankroll, fee drag is manageable (4–8% of gross EV). At live capital, T1 fees scale proportionally with Kelly stakes; T4 fees are fixed per game regardless of bankroll. Before ADR-0005 is accepted, the break-even analysis must confirm that expected EV vs. real market prices (EXP-017, GG-004) exceeds the fee floor at the intended live bankroll size. This analysis provides the fee cost structure; EXP-017 must supply the EV numerator. GG-019 Part A (code bug) resolved by T-149. GG-019 Part B (aggregate cost governance) CLOSED — analysis documented here and in GG-019 entry. Both: resolved pending ADR-0005 acceptance. | DS |

---

## Decision Principles

These principles guide all decisions in this project:

1. **No decision is reversible without a superseding ADR.** ADRs are immutable once accepted.
2. **"We'll fix it later" is a shadow commitment.** If it matters, log it as an open question or RID.
3. **Deferred decisions must have a named gate.** "TBD" without a deadline is a gap, not a decision.
4. **arb/ folder: gate is APR-0010, not momentum.** No incorporation without explicit approval.
5. **Live capital decisions require two artifacts:** a superseding ADR and a signed approval (APR-0009).

| 2026-06-10 | **[SESSION 46 / ADR-0021 Incremental Delta Materiality — GG-025 architectural fix, ADR-0020 D2 superseded]** DS overrode GG-025's post-G6 deferral: "We don't want a band-aid — the architecture needs the correct fix... the recommended values cannot be arbitrary and... [must be] situationally agnostic to execute on edge cases — but structurally sound to not fall apart in the aggregate." **The fix:** materiality is now judged on `delta_p_incr = p_t4 − p_t4_baseline` where `p_t4_baseline = p_exceeds_threshold(0, threshold, pace×48)` — both terms from the same raw-Poisson model, so the p_t4-vs-p_t1 calibration gap (mean 0.376 at tip-off) cancels algebraically rather than being suppressed by a fitted constant. Improvement over the GG-025 registered design: no stored game-start snapshot — baseline is deterministic from pregame pace (pts_l10_mean/36), stateless and restart-safe (G4's mid-game T4 process death would have corrupted a snapshot design). **The gate:** `|delta_p_incr| ≥ max(T4_DELTA_SIGMA_Z·σ_null(t), T4_MIN_DELTA_FLOOR)` where σ_null(t) is the EXACT null std-dev of delta_p_incr at elapsed time t, computed by Poisson pmf enumeration. By Poisson additivity E[delta_p_incr]=0 under the null (verified to 1e-9). Structural invariants tested: σ_null(tip-off)=0; σ_null(final horn)=√(b(1−b)); monotone non-decreasing (martingale variance). **Constants (zero fitted to G4):** Z=2.0 (EXP-019A house σ-convention); floor=0.04 (mirrors MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE, ADR-0020 D1 — sub-economic moves can't trade regardless of significance); GAME_MINUTES_TOTAL=48. Edge cases: pace=0 → σ=0 floor governs; t_rem>48 or <0 clamped; pmf tail bounded at Bernoulli max. **G4 replay (n=2,193):** materiality pass rate 74.6%→9.7%; Q1 89.3%→10.8% (calibration flood gone); Q2 55.2%→6.8%; Q4 1.4%→13.5% (sensitivity GAINED where live signal accumulates — proves bias cancelled, not signal suppressed; fixed 0.50 would have killed both). T4_MIN_DELTA_P removed; T4Signal carries p_t4_baseline/delta_p_incr/sigma_null, all logged to t4_position_trajectories.jsonl for G5 empirical validation. Fire condition unchanged (`is_material AND p_t4 > mip+vig`); exit mechanics (ADR-0019) untouched. Tests: 830/830 (10 new σ_null invariant tests, 5 new gate tests; 2 TestMinBetFloor fixtures updated edge 0.03→0.05 — ADR-0020 MIN_EDGE=0.04 casualty, floor logic itself unchanged). Review trigger: post-G5 replay of logged delta_p_incr vs realized outcomes; if fired signals show no realized edge vs non-fired, escalate to DS (GG-004 standard applies to live signal too). GG-025 RESOLVED. | DS |

| 2026-06-10 | **[SESSION 46 / ADR-0022 T-161 ruling: T4 budget realized-value exit credit — GG-023 RESOLVED]** DS directive: "address the T-161 DS Ruling, we need a reset so the strip runs clean on potentially the last game of the season." **Ruling: option (c), realized-value credit, capped at entry cost.** Rejected: (a) revolving credit — a losing exit would free more capacity than the cash that returned, making gross deployment unbounded in a high-churn losing game and reducing the band budget to a non-constraint (losses bounded only by the daily-loss switch); (b) gross commitment cap (status quo) — the G4 failure: $500 pool pinned at 100% before halftime, 29 exits freed capital, `fired=0` all second half, T-152 evidence halved. **Mechanics:** `deployed = open T4 exposure + realized T4 losses this game`. On exit, `credit = size_usd + min(0, exit_pnl)` (`_exit_budget_credit()`): win → entry cost only (capacity never exceeds the authorized budget — wins don't expand risk authorization); loss → entry cost + pnl (losses permanently consume budget). **Invariants (test-enforced):** aggregate band net loss ≤ t4_band_budget (true capital constraint restored); no starvation (capacity recycles — 100 winning round-trips on a 20-concurrent budget all fire); deployed ≥ 0; restart-lossless. **Restart safety:** `_reconstruct_t4_ledger(portfolio, game_date)` rebuilds the ledger at process start from open pool_isolated exposure + per-exit realized losses (wins don't offset other positions' losses — identical arithmetic to the incremental path; G4's mid-game T4 process death would otherwise have authorized a second full budget). **G5 clean strip:** ledger keyed to game_date — G5 starts at $0.00 deployed automatically; G4's unresolved open positions cannot contaminate G5 capacity; no manual state surgery. **Coherence:** daily-loss kill switch (ADR-0012 net semantics) independent and unchanged — band ledger uses per-exit loss accounting (capital authorization), daily-loss uses net (loss tolerance); both tiers fire independently per CLAUDE.md rule 9 / GG-010. BAND_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED halt unchanged, now triggered by true consumed capital. Review trigger: post-G5, if exhaustion still occurs early with ADR-0021's ~10% fire volume, escalate pool sizing to DS. 13 new tests (test_t161_budget_credit.py); 843/843. ADR-0022 ACCEPTED (amends ADR-0013 ledger semantics only). | DS |

ALL open positions — including positions where the Kalshi order failed — now block re-entry on the same ticker. The second cron window is a connectivity redundancy guard, not a re-evaluation window that should re-price bets on a smaller bankroll. The prior "failed = transient = retry" logic was the root cause of 12 duplicate G5 bets (including Wemby NO ≥35 and NO ≥40 double-deployed). **(2) T-164 — Primary cron moved 22:00 UTC → 18:00 UTC (`deploy/cron.conf`):** Four entries updated together — health check (21:00→17:00), T-140 cache (21:25→17:25), PIPE-02 primary (21:55→17:55), PIPE-03 primary (22:00→18:00). Late window unchanged (01:30 UTC). Root cause is server-side Kalshi API congestion at ~22:00 UTC basketball rush hour — T-164 Part 1 (inter-order sleep + retry count) addressed self-generated rate pressure, which was not the binding constraint. G5 confirmed 01:00 UTC off-peak batch = 0% failure rate vs 22:00 UTC = 31% failure rate (unchanged post-T-164). 18:00 UTC = 2pm ET is pre-rush with identical off-peak behavior expected. Also synced `PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING=true` to local `cron.conf` (was live on VPS but missing from repo). **(3) T-167 — MC script rewrite (`scripts/run_monte_carlo.py`):** Portfolio mode (default, no flag): reads `paper_portfolio.json`, simulates from committed positions using `model_p`, `direction`, `mid_price_at_entry`, `size_usd` with Bernoulli draws per position. Preview mode (`--preview`): reads pregame JSON (pre-deployment simulation, old default behavior). Scenario flags: `--exclude-failed` (Kalshi-confirmed only), `--yes-only` / `--no-only` (EXP-018 direction split), `--haircut PCT` (T-153 integration point). Constants corrected to production values: `DEFAULT_T1_KELLY=0.25`, `DEFAULT_MIN_EDGE=0.04` (were 0.10/0.02, stale from ADR-0010 era). **(4) DS architectural ruling — no per-player exposure caps:** DS explicitly ruled no `max_player_exposure_usd` or `max_player_pct_of_bankroll` in `_HARD_LIMITS`. Wemby concentration in G5 ($323.33 / 32% of portfolio) is structurally expected at single-game Finals scale. Regular-season dilution (15+ games, 300+ markets) is endogenous to market structure — no explicit per-player cap needed. The architecture is structurally sound; ADR-0005 (live capital framework, Phase 2) is the correct venue for live capital concentration parameters. | DS |

KILL_SWITCH path intentionally left without pam_archetype — kill switch fires before player context is relevant. Design rationale for best-effort pattern: T4 must never stall on a data-enrichment lookup; archetype is observational context, not a routing input. **(4) GG-027 registered:** PAM wired to T1 (model features + audit log) and T4 (decision log + routing result) only. T2/T3/T5 bands have no archetype wiring. T4 archetype does not affect routing math. EnvironmentClassifier (pressure-context game-environment categorizer — elimination_game, series_momentum, crowd_premium, role_expansion_flag → environment_class HIGH_PRESSURE / NEUTRAL / LOW_STAKES) is not built. QS snapshot dated 2026-05-28 not updated for Finals role changes (e.g., Alvarado role expansion). Combined: LOW severity now; HIGH pre-MLB activation. **(5) T-165 registered (MLB gate):** Full PAM integration scope: (a) EnvironmentClassifier in C2; (b) archetype × environment uncertainty multiplier in C3; (c) dynamic MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE per environment class; (d) T2/T3/T5 archetype register wiring. | 2026-06-12 | **[SESSION 55] T-168 (GG-030 RESOLVED): MC correlated simulation fix; GG-031/032 registered; T-170 (T4 ledger correction) RESOLVED** | Four decisions. **(1) T-168 — `simulate_portfolio()` Poisson correlation fix (`scripts/run_monte_carlo.py`, GG-030 RESOLVED):** T-167's MC rewrite (SESSION 53) fixed data sourcing (reads committed positions from `paper_portfolio.json`) but regressed the simulation's correlation structure. It drew independent Bernoulli per position (`rng.random(n_sims) < b["p_win"]`), treating threshold ladders on the same player as independent events. They are not — Wemby's NO ≥35, NO ≥40 both resolve against the same game score. Preview mode (`--preview`) already drew correlated results correctly via Poisson simulation. Fix: `simulate_portfolio()` now matches preview mode. One Poisson score drawn per player per simulation. Lambda inferred via `_infer_lambda()` — least-squares fit to the player's `(threshold, model_p)` pairs using `scipy.optimize.minimize_scalar` with bounds (0.1, 80), where `model_p = P(score ≥ threshold | lambda)` regardless of bet direction. Direction-invariant: `_infer_lambda()` always uses raw `b["model_p"]` — the first implementation used `1.0 - b["model_p"]` for NO bets, inverting the Poisson distribution and causing NO bets with model_p=0.001 (99.9% p_win) to infer an extremely high lambda, reversing all win/loss flags (MC output: "avg bets won: 0.0, P(profit): 0.0%"). Fixed: always use raw model_p. Governance footer updated: GG-030 resolved note replaces "independent Bernoulli" disclaimer. **(2) GG-031 registered (GG-028 blast radius understated):** SESSION 53 and 52 documented 2 duplicated tickers from GG-028. Direct VPS audit of all 41 positions (this session) found 9 duplicated (ticker, direction) pairs — 40% of total deployment ($402.49 doubled exposure), not 2. All 9 follow the same pattern: one `failed: transient` (18:0x UTC primary batch) + one `resting` (01:30 UTC late batch). The "mystery 01:00 UTC trigger" identified in prior sessions is the scheduled 01:30 UTC late PIPE-03 cron window — not a separate trigger. T-166's idempotency fix correctly prevents recurrence. T-169 registered: DS ruling on 9 twins needed — annotate as `duplicate_of` (Option A) or accept as double exposure (Option B). **(3) GG-032 registered (T4 bankroll_usd irreconcilable):** `paper_portfolio_t4.json` header had `bankroll_usd=$1,869.68`, `initial_bankroll_usd=$1,000.00`, `total_realized_pnl=-$21.59`. Root cause: ADR-0022's `_reconstruct_t4_ledger()` wrote the T1 system bankroll (~$1,869) into T4's `bankroll_usd` field — wrong object, wrong field. True T4 pool value: $500 initial + (-$21.59 realized) = $478.41. Prior claim of "+87% T4 growth" was based on comparing $1,869 bankroll to $1,000 initial — both wrong fields. T-170 registered. **(4) T-170 RESOLVED — T4 ledger corrected on VPS:** `paper_portfolio_t4.json` fields corrected: `bankroll_usd` 1869.68→478.41, `initial_bankroll_usd` 1000.0→500.0. `_t170_correction_note` added to file documenting derivation. Backup written to `logs/paper_portfolio_t4.json.bak_t170` before edit. `total_realized_pnl=-21.59` unchanged (correct). **(5) G5 MC output (correlated simulation):** 41 positions, $1,003.38 deployed, bankroll $130.62.
